=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an m x n grid. A robot starts at the top-left corner of the grid (0, 0) and wants to reach the bottom-right corner (m - 1, n - 1). The robot can move either right or down at any point in time.
The grid contains a value coins[i][j] in each cell:

If coins[i][j] >= 0, the robot gains that many coins.
If coins[i][j] < 0, the robot encounters a robber, and the robber steals the absolute value of coins[i][j] coins.

The robot has a special ability to neutralize robbers in at most 2 cells on its path, preventing them from stealing coins in those cells.
Note: The robot's total coins can be negative.
Return the maximum profit the robot can gain on the route.
 
Example 1:

Input: coins = [[0,1,-1],[1,-2,3],[2,-3,4]]
Output: 8
Explanation:
An optimal path for maximum coins is:

Start at (0, 0) with 0 coins (total coins = 0).
Move to (0, 1), gaining 1 coin (total coins = 0 + 1 = 1).
Move to (1, 1), where there's a robber stealing 2 coins. The robot uses one neutralization here, avoiding the robbery (total coins = 1).
Move to (1, 2), gaining 3 coins (total coins = 1 + 3 = 4).
Move to (2, 2), gaining 4 coins (total coins = 4 + 4 = 8).


Example 2:

Input: coins = [[10,10,10],[10,10,10]]
Output: 40
Explanation:
An optimal path for maximum coins is:

Start at (0, 0) with 10 coins (total coins = 10).
Move to (0, 1), gaining 10 coins (total coins = 10 + 10 = 20).
Move to (0, 2), gaining another 10 coins (total coins = 20 + 10 = 30).
Move to (1, 2), gaining the final 10 coins (total coins = 30 + 10 = 40).


 
Constraints:

m == coins.length
n == coins[i].length
1 <= m, n <= 500
-1000 <= coins[i][j] <= 1000

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maximumAmount(self, coins: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Maybe they treat k as number of neutralizations used? Let's think.

If exact k (number used so far), then at start:
- If coins[0][0] >=0: only k=0 possible, dp[0][0][0]=coin, dp[0][0][1]=dp[0][0][2]=-inf.
- If coins[0][0] <0: dp[0][0][0]=coin (not neutralize), dp[0][0][1]=0 (neutralize), dp[0][0][2]=-inf (can't use 2 on one cell).
Then transitions:
For nonnegative: dp[k] = prev[k] + coin (no neutralization). For negative: dp[k] = max(prev[k] + coin, prev[k-1] if k>0). End max dp[0..2]. This exact state is also correct. It may be more intuitive and avoids at-most monotonic. But initialization slightly more complex. Let's compare.

At-most state:
- dp[k] = max profit using at most k neutralizations.
- Initialize start negative: dp[0]=coin, dp[1]=0, dp[2]=0. Start nonnegative: all coin.
- Transition as above.
This is correct and simpler to initialize. But need be careful in final answer: dp[2] at start negative =0, which is same as dp[1]; no issue. At-most state may be less standard but accepted.

Exact state:
- dp[k] = max profit using exactly k neutralizations.
- Initialize start:
  if coin >=0: dp[0]=coin; dp[1]=dp[2]=NEG
  else: dp[0]=coin; dp[1]=0; dp[2]=NEG
- For each cell:
  if coin >=0:
    dp[k] = best_prev[k] + coin (no neutralization)
  else:
    dp[k] = max(best_prev[k] + coin, best_prev[k-1] if k>0)
- End max(dp[0..2]).
This is also simple. But if using exact, for nonnegative start, dp[1]=NEG, so later if path has one negative and no previous neutralization, dp[1] can be achieved by neutralizing current from prev[0]. Good. If path has no negative, dp[1] remains NEG, but final max dp0 works. At-most final dp2 also works. Exact may be more robust because "at most 2" final max. But initialization exact with dp[2]=NEG for start negative: if there is only start cell and negative, final max dp1=0, answer 0. Good. cell -1:

k1 = max(prev1 + (-1) = -1, prev0 = -1) = -1.
k2 = max(prev2 + (-1) = NEG, prev1 = 0) = 0. Good. Answer max 0. Good.

Let's consider if we can simplify further using 3 rolling arrays:
NEG = -10**18
dp0 = [NEG]*n
dp1 = [NEG]*n
dp2 = [NEG]*n
for i in range(m):
  for j in range(n):
    if i==0 and j==0:
       if coins[0][0] >=0:
          dp0[j]=dp1[j]=dp2[j]=coins[0][0]
       else:
          dp0[j]=coins[0][0]; dp1[j]=dp2[j]=0
       continue
    best0 = dp0[j] if i>0? Wait rolling row: Need current row values for left and previous row values for top. If using arrays updated in place, dp0[j] before update is top (previous row) for current j, and dp0[j-1] after update is left. So:
top0 = dp0[j] (old previous row)
left0 = dp0[j-1] (current row updated)
best0 = max(top0, left0)
Similarly for k=1,2.
But need be careful when j=0: left NEG. For i=0, top NEG. For start skip. This rolling O(n) memory is efficient and not too complex. Need handle negative transitions with prev_best[k-1] from top/left. Since arrays for k-1 also have old top at j and new left at j-1. We can compute.

Let's design rolling DP with at-most state.

Pseudo:
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumAmount(self, coins: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(coins), len(coins[0])
        NEG = -10**18
        dp0 = [NEG] * n
        dp1 = [NEG] * n
        dp2 = [NEG] * n

        for i in range(m):
            for j in range(n):
                val = coins[i][j]
                if i == 0 and j == 0:
                    if val >= 0:
                        dp0[j] = dp1[j] = dp2[j] = val
                    else:
                        dp0[j] = val
                        dp1[j] = 0
                        dp2[j] = 0
                    continue

                # previous row top values are still in arrays at j (not overwritten yet)
                # left values are in arrays at j-1 (already overwritten for current row)
                # For each k, best previous with at most k neutralizations:
                best0 = NEG
                if i > 0: best0 = max(best0, dp0[j])  # top old
                if j > 0: best0 = max(best0, dp0[j-1]) # left new
                best1 = NEG
                if i > 0: best1 = max(best1, dp1[j])
                if j > 0: best1 = max(best1, dp1[j-1])
                best2 = NEG
                if i > 0: best2 = max(best2, dp2[j])
                if j > 0: best2 = max(best2, dp2[j-1])

                if val >= 0:
                    dp0[j] = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG
                    dp1[j] = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
                    dp2[j] = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
                else:
                    # not neutralize
                    new0 = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG
                    # k=1: max(not neutralize from best1, neutralize from best0)
                    new1 = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
                    if best0 != NEG:
                        new1 = max(new1, best0)
                    # k=2: max(not neutralize from best2, neutralize from best1)
                    new2 = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
                    if best1 != NEG:
                        new2 = max(new2, best1)
                    dp0[j], dp1[j], dp2[j] = new0, new1, new2
        return dp2[n-1]  # at most 2
But wait: In rolling arrays, for current cell, dp0[j] is old top. But after we compute dp0[j], we overwrite. For computing dp1[j], we need best1 top from dp1[j] old. That's okay. For computing dp2[j], need best2 top from dp2[j] old. We compute all bests before overwriting any? In code above, we compute best0,best1,best2 before overwriting. Good. But note when computing best1, dp1[j] old top; dp1[j-1] new left. Good. When computing best2, dp2[j] old top; dp2[j-1] new left. Good. Then assign dp0[j], dp1[j], dp2[j]. However, if we assign dp0[j] before computing best1? We compute all first, so okay. But after assigning dp0[j], dp0[j] no longer top for next row? For next row, dp0[j] will be top for row i+1, which is correct after current row computed. For same row next j+1, dp0[j] is left, correct. Good.

Need be careful with j=0 and i>0: dp0[0] old top from previous row; no left. We overwrite dp0[0]. For next row, dp0[0] top from current row, correct. For j=0, dp0[j-1] not used. Good.

Potential issue: For i=0, j>0, top values dp0[j] are NEG (initialized), left values dp0[j-1] are current row. Good. For j=0, i>0, top values dp0[0] from previous row, left NEG. Good.

No, at-most 2, using one is at most2; best2+val with best2>=best0 captures not neutralizing. If best2 is NEG? But best2 should be reachable. If best2 not NEG, best2+val >= best0+val? Since best2>=best0. So best0+val not needed. If best2 is NEG but best0 not? Can that happen? At-most states monotonic, so no. But due initialization maybe? Let's verify. At-most state with rolling arrays also can use guards. But exact state maybe requires final max. Let's compare code length.

Exact rolling code:
NEG = -10**18
dp0 = [NEG]*n; dp1=[NEG]*n; dp2=[NEG]*n
for i in range(m):
    for j in range(n):
        val = coins[i][j]
        if i == 0 and j == 0:
            if val >= 0:
                dp0[j] = val
                dp1[j] = NEG
                dp2[j] = NEG
            else:
                dp0[j] = val
                dp1[j] = 0
                dp2[j] = NEG
            continue

        top0 = dp0[j] if i > 0 else NEG
        left0 = dp0[j-1] if j > 0 else NEG
        best0 = top0 if top0 > left0 else left0
        top1 = dp1[j] if i > 0 else NEG
        left1 = dp1[j-1] if j > 0 else NEG
        best1 = top1 if top1 > left1 else left1
        top2 = dp2[j] if i > 0 else NEG
        left2 = dp2[j-1] if j > 0 else NEG
        best2 = top2 if top2 > left2 else left2

        if val >= 0:
            dp0[j] = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG
            dp1[j] = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
            dp2[j] = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
        else:
            new0 = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG
            new1 = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
            if best0 != NEG and best0 > new1:
                new1 = best0
            new2 = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
            if best1 != NEG and best1 > new2:
                new2 = best1
            dp0[j], dp1[j], dp2[j] = new0, new1, new2
return max(dp0[n-1], dp1[n-1], dp2[n-1])

At-most rolling code:
Init start:
if val >=0: dp0=dp1=dp2=val
else: dp0=val; dp1=dp2=0
Transitions:
if val >=0:
 dp0=best0+val; dp1=best1+val; dp2=best2+val
else:
 new0=best0+val
 new1=max(best1+val, best0)
 new2=max(best2+val, best1)
return dp2[n-1]

At-most code is shorter and no final max. But exact state may be more semantically precise. Which should final answer use? Both accepted. Need decide. The problem statement says "at most 2 cells". At-most state directly models that. I think at-most is elegant. Need ensure no hidden issue with "neutralize robbers in at most 2 cells" and "at most k" state where dp[k] is max with at most k neutralizations. Let's prove transition for negative:
newdp[k] = max(prev[k] + coin, prev[k-1])
But wait: If previous path used at most k-1 and current negative and we do NOT neutralize, total used at most k-1, which is at most k. This is captured by prev[k] + coin because prev[k] >= prev[k-1]. But our DP state dp[k] is max over at most k, and we maintain monotonic. If for some reason due NEG, prev[k] might not be >= prev[k-1]? It should. Let's verify with exact? At-most by definition. Our initialization and transitions preserve. Let's prove:
Base: start nonnegative: dp0=dp1=dp2=coin => nondecreasing. start negative: dp0=coin<0, dp1=dp2=0 => nondecreasing.
Induction: Suppose prev0<=prev1<=prev2.
For nonnegative val:
new0=prev0+val, new1=prev1+val, new2=prev2+val => nondecreasing.
For negative val:
new0=prev0+val.
new1=max(prev1+val, prev0). Since prev1>=prev0, prev1+val >= prev0+val. But is prev1+val >= prev0? Not necessarily? Example prev0=100, prev1=101, val=-200 => prev1+val=-99 < prev0=100. But new1=max(-99,100)=100. new0=100-200=-100. new1>=new0. Good.
new2=max(prev2+val, prev1). Need new2>=new1. Since prev2>=prev1, prev2+val >= prev1+val. Also prev1 >= max(prev1+val, prev0)? Since val negative, prev1 > prev1+val, and prev1>=prev0. So prev1 >= new1. Thus new2>=new1. So monotonic. Good.
Thus prev[k] includes prev[k-1] paths. So at-most transition correct.

Potential issue: For negative cell, newdp[k] = max(prev[k] + coin, prev[k-1]) where prev[k-1] is at most k-1. But if previous path used at most k-1 and current negative and we neutralize, total used at most k. Good. If previous path used at most k and current negative and we neutralize, total used at most k+1, not allowed. If previous path used at most k-2 and current negative and we somehow use two neutralizations? No, one cell can be neutralized once. Not allowed. Good.

At-most state final answer dp2. If dp2 at-most includes paths using 0,1,2. Good.

Let's read problem statement carefully: "You are given an m x n grid. A robot starts at the top-left corner of the grid (0, 0) and wants to reach the bottom-right corner (m - 1, n - 1). The robot can move either right or down at any point in time.
The grid contains a value coins[i][j] in each cell:

If coins[i][j] >= 0, the robot gains that many coins.
If coins[i][j] < 0, the robot encounters a robber, and the robber steals the absolute value of coins[i][j] coins.

The robot has a special ability to neutralize robbers in at most 2 cells on its path, preventing them from stealing coins in those cells.
Note: The robot's total coins can be negative.
Return the maximum profit the robot can gain on the route."

"at most 2 cells on its path" Could the robot neutralize a robber in a cell with negative value and then still gain? No, "preventing them from stealing coins" means no loss, not gain. So add 0. Good.

"maximum profit" maybe if total coins can be negative, profit can be negative. We maximize.

Need maybe if coins[i][j] = 0, nonnegative, gain 0. No robber. Neutralization not needed. Good.

Example2 says "Start at (0,0) with 10 coins" because coins[0][0]=10. So yes include start cell. DP includes start.

Let's decide final code. I lean at-most rolling DP for concise. Need ensure code is clear enough. Let's write final code:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumAmount(self, coins: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(coins), len(coins[0])
        NEG = -10**18

        dp0 = [NEG] * n
        dp1 = [NEG] * n
        dp2 = [NEG] * n

        for i in range(m):
            for j in range(n):
                val = coins[i][j]

                if i == 0 and j == 0:
                    if val >= 0:
                        dp0[j] = dp1[j] = dp2[j] = val
                    else:
                        dp0[j] = val
                        dp1[j] = 0
                        dp2[j] = 0
                    continue

                best0 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best0 = dp0[j]
                if j > 0:
                    if dp0[j - 1] > best0:
                        best0 = dp0[j - 1]

                best1 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best1 = dp1[j]
                if j > 0:
                    if dp1[j - 1] > best1:
                        best1 = dp1[j - 1]

                best2 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best2 = dp2[j]
                if j > 0:
                    if dp2[j - 1] > best2:
                        best2 = dp2[j - 1]

                if val >= 0:
                    dp0[j] = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG
                    dp1[j] = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
                    dp2[j] = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
                else:
                    new0 = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG

                    new1 = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
                    if best0 != NEG and best0 > new1:
                        new1 = best0

                    new2 = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
                    if best1 != NEG and best1 > new2:
                        new2 = best1

                    dp0[j] = new0
                    dp1[j] = new1
                    dp2[j] = new2

        return dp2[n - 1]

```
dp0 = [NEG]*n
dp1 = [NEG]*n
dp2 = [NEG]*n
for i...
  if start:
    if val >=0:
      dp0[j]=val; dp1[j]=NEG; dp2[j]=NEG
    else:
      dp0[j]=val; dp1[j]=0; dp2[j]=NEG
    continue
  best0...
  if val >=0:
    dp0[j] = best0+val if best0 != NEG else NEG
    dp1[j] = best1+val if best1 != NEG else NEG
    dp2[j] = best2+val if best2 != NEG else NEG
  else:
    new0 = best0+val if best0 != NEG else NEG
    new1 = best1+val if best1 != NEG else NEG
    if best0 != NEG and best0 > new1: new1 = best0
    new2 = best2+val if best2 != NEG else NEG
    if best1 != NEG and best1 > new2: new2 = best1
    ...
return max(dp0[n-1], dp1[n-1], dp2[n-1])
```

For more than two, dp2 represents using exactly 2 neutralizations (not more), which is allowed because at most 2; final max includes dp2. Good.

Let's maybe write a proof for myself:
Let dp[k][i][j] be max coins after reaching (i,j) having neutralized at most k robbers along the path. For current cell:
- If coins[i][j] >= 0: no robber, so state k comes from previous cell with same k plus coin.
- If coins[i][j] < 0: either don't neutralize, state k from previous same k plus coin (negative), or neutralize, state k from previous k-1 plus 0.
Because dp[k] is at most k, previous dp[k] includes paths with fewer than k neutralizations, so not neutralizing with fewer than k is covered.
This is correct.

For a later nonnegative after one negative, new2=best2+val. If best2=NEG, new2=NEG. Good. This exact state is straightforward. Exact state rolling code:
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumAmount(self, coins: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(coins), len(coins[0])
        NEG = -10**18
        dp0 = [NEG] * n
        dp1 = [NEG] * n
        dp2 = [NEG] * n

        for i in range(m):
            for j in range(n):
                val = coins[i][j]
                if i == 0 and j == 0:
                    if val >= 0:
                        dp0[j] = val
                        dp1[j] = NEG
                        dp2[j] = NEG
                    else:
                        dp0[j] = val
                        dp1[j] = 0
                        dp2[j] = NEG
                    continue

                best0 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best0 = dp0[j]
                if j > 0 and dp0[j-1] > best0:
                    best0 = dp0[j-1]
                best1 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best1 = dp1[j]
                if j > 0 and dp1[j-1] > best1:
                    best1 = dp1[j-1]
                best2 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best2 = dp2[j]
                if j > 0 and dp2[j-1] > best2:
                    best2 = dp2[j-1]

                if val >= 0:
                    dp0[j] = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG
                    dp1[j] = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
                    dp2[j] = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
                else:
                    new0 = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG
                    new1 = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
                    if best0 != NEG and best0 > new1:
                        new1 = best0
                    new2 = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
                    if best1 != NEG and best1 > new2:
                        new2 = best1
                    dp0[j] = new0
                    dp1[j] = new1
                    dp2[j] = new2

        return max(dp0[n-1], dp1[n-1], dp2[n-1])
```
This exact code is correct. Let's test with example1 exact earlier gave answer 8. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is a more direct DP with `dp[k][i][j]` and exact state but no rolling. Rolling code can be harder to read. But final code can be 3D for clarity. Which is better for final answer? The constraints m,n 500, 3D memory okay. 3D code simpler to understand and less prone to rolling bugs. Let's evaluate.

3D exact code:
```
dp = [[[NEG]*3 for _ in range(n)] for _ in range(m)]
for i in range(m):
    for j in range(n):
        if i==0 and j==0:
            if coins[i][j] >=0:
                dp[i][j][0]=coins[i][j]
                dp[i][j][1]=NEG
                dp[i][j][2]=NEG
            else:
                dp[i][j][0]=coins[i][j]
                dp[i][j][1]=0
                dp[i][j][2]=NEG
            continue
        for k in range(3):
            best = NEG
            if i>0: best=max(best, dp[i-1][j][k])
            if j>0: best=max(best, dp[i][j-1][k])
            if coins[i][j] >=0:
                if best != NEG:
                    dp[i][j][k] = best + coins[i][j]
            else:
                if best != NEG:
                    dp[i][j][k] = best + coins[i][j]
                if k>0:
                    prev_best = NEG
                    if i>0: prev_best=max(prev_best, dp[i-1][j][k-1])
                    if j>0: prev_best=max(prev_best, dp[i][j-1][k-1])
                    if prev_best != NEG:
                        dp[i][j][k] = max(dp[i][j][k], prev_best)
return max(dp[m-1][n-1])
```
This is very clear and less bug-prone. Memory: dp is m x n x 3. m,n=500 => 250k cells * 3 = 750k ints. In Python, list of lists of lists: outer 500 lists, each inner 500 lists of 3. Each inner list object overhead ~56 bytes, 250k*56=14MB; references 250k*3*8=6MB; ints maybe many NEG references initially, but updated to new ints for each state? Actually each state assigned an int, so 750k int objects ~21MB. Total ~41MB plus outer lists. LeetCode memory limit maybe 256MB, okay. Time: loops 250k * 3 =750k, each with max and indexing, okay. 3D code maybe accepted. Rolling code O(n) memory but more complex. Which should final answer use? The problem constraints m,n 500, 3D memory okay. But if memory limit is 64MB? 41MB might be close. LeetCode typical memory 256MB. But to be safe, rolling O(n) is better. The user asked competitive programming; O(n) memory is elegant. But final code with rolling arrays is a bit more complex. Need ensure no bug. I can write rolling exact code cleanly. Let's maybe use rolling exact code to be safe memory and still correct.

Let's think if there is any issue with rolling exact code and `best1` computed before overwriting `dp1[j]` but after overwriting `dp1[j-1]`. Yes. But in code, if we compute `best0`, then `best1`, then `best2`, and then assign `dp0[j]`, `dp1[j]`, `dp2[j]`. We compute all bests before assignments. Good. Need not worry that `dp0[j]` assignment affects `best1`? No, best1 uses dp1, not dp0. But if we assign dp0[j] before computing best1, dp0[j] no longer top but best1 doesn't need dp0. Rolling exact with list of 3 arrays:
```
dp = [[NEG]*n for _ in range(3)]
for i in range(m):
    for j in range(n):
        val = coins[i][j]
        if i == 0 and j == 0:
            if val >= 0:
                dp[0][j] = val
                dp[1][j] = NEG
                dp[2][j] = NEG
            else:
                dp[0][j] = val
                dp[1][j] = 0
                dp[2][j] = NEG
            continue
        best = [NEG, NEG, NEG]
        for k in range(3):
            if i > 0:
                best[k] = dp[k][j]
            if j > 0 and dp[k][j-1] > best[k]:
                best[k] = dp[k][j-1]
        if val >= 0:
            for k in range(3):
                dp[k][j] = best[k] + val if best[k] != NEG else NEG
        else:
            new0 = best[0] + val if best[0] != NEG else NEG
            new1 = best[1] + val if best[1] != NEG else NEG
            if best[0] != NEG and best[0] > new1:
                new1 = best[0]
            new2 = best[2] + val if best[2] != NEG else NEG
            if best[1] != NEG and best[1] > new2:
                new2 = best[1]
            dp[0][j] = new0
            dp[1][j] = new1
            dp[2][j] = new2
return max(dp[0][n-1], dp[1][n-1], dp[2][n-1])
```
This is more compact. But note: In the `for k in range(3)` for best, when k=0, `dp[0][j-1]` is current row left. When k=1, `dp[1][j-1]` current row left. Good. But we compute best for all k before assigning any dp[k][j]. Good. This code is clean. However, for val>=0, we assign dp[0][j], dp[1][j], dp[2][j] in a loop. For val<0, assign individually. Good.

Potential issue: In exact state, if val>=0 and best[k] is NEG, dp[k][j] = NEG. But if best[k] is NEG and val positive, NEG + val would be less than NEG; guard. Good.

Potential issue: In exact state, if val<0 and best[1] is NEG but best[0] reachable, new1=best0. Good. If best[0] is NEG but best[1] reachable, new1=best1+val. Good. If both reachable, max. Good.

Potential issue: In exact state, if val<0 and best[2] is NEG but best[1] reachable, new2=best1. If best[1] is NEG but best[2] reachable, new2=best2+val. Good.

This list-of-arrays code is concise. But variable `dp` is list of arrays; `dp[k][j]` top old, `dp[k][j-1]` left new. Good.

Let's write final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumAmount(self, coins: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(coins), len(coins[0])
        NEG = -10**18

        # dp[k][j] = best profit reaching current row, column j,
        # having used exactly k neutralizations.
        dp = [[NEG] * n for _ in range(3)]

        for i in range(m):
            for j in range(n):
                val = coins[i][j]

                if i == 0 and j == 0:
                    if val >= 0:
                        dp[0][j] = val
                        dp[1][j] = NEG
                        dp[2][j] = NEG
                    else:
                        dp[0][j] = val
                        dp[1][j] = 0
                        dp[2][j] = NEG
                    continue

                best = [NEG, NEG, NEG]
                for k in range(3):
                    if i > 0:
                        best[k] = dp[k][j]
                    if j > 0 and dp[k][j - 1] > best[k]:
                        best[k] = dp[k][j - 1]

                if val >= 0:
                    for k in range(3):
                        if best[k] != NEG:
                            dp[k][j] = best[k] + val
                        else:
                            dp[k][j] = NEG
                else:
                    new0 = best[0] + val if best[0] != NEG else NEG

                    new1 = best[1] + val if best[1] != NEG else NEG
                    if best[0] != NEG and best[0] > new1:
                        new1 = best[0]

                    new2 = best[2] + val if best[2] != NEG else NEG
                    if best[1] != NEG and best[1] > new2:
                        new2 = best[1]

                    dp[0][j] = new0
                    dp[1][j] = new1
                    dp[2][j] = new2

        return max(dp[0][n - 1], dp[1][n - 1], dp[2][n - 1])
```

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumAmount(self, coins: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(coins), len(coins[0])
        NEG = -10**18

        # dp[k][j]: best profit at the current row, column j,
        # after using exactly k neutralizations.
        dp = [[NEG] * n for _ in range(3)]

        for i in range(m):
            for j in range(n):
                val = coins[i][j]

                if i == 0 and j == 0:
                    if val >= 0:
                        dp[0][j] = val
                        dp[1][j] = NEG
                        dp[2][j] = NEG
                    else:
                        dp[0][j] = val
                        dp[1][j] = 0
                        dp[2][j] = NEG
                    continue

                best0 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best0 = dp[0][j]
                if j > 0 and dp[0][j - 1] > best0:
                    best0 = dp[0][j - 1]

                best1 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best1 = dp[1][j]
                if j > 0 and dp[1][j - 1] > best1:
                    best1 = dp[1][j - 1]

                best2 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best2 = dp[2][j]
                if j > 0 and dp[2][j - 1] > best2:
                    best2 = dp[2][j - 1]

                if val >= 0:
                    dp[0][j] = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG
                    dp[1][j] = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
                    dp[2][j] = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
                else:
                    new0 = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG

                    new1 = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
                    if best0 != NEG and best0 > new1:
                        new1 = best0

                    new2 = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
                    if best1 != NEG and best1 > new2:
                        new2 = best1

                    dp[0][j] = new0
                    dp[1][j] = new1
                    dp[2][j] = new2

        return max(dp[0][n - 1], dp[1][n - 1], dp[2][n - 1])
```

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumAmount(self, coins: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(coins), len(coins[0])
        NEG = -10**18
        dp = [[NEG] * n for _ in range(3)]
        for i in range(m):
            for j in range(n):
                val = coins[i][j]
                if i == 0 and j == 0:
                    if val >= 0:
                        dp[0][j] = dp[1][j] = dp[2][j] = val
                    else:
                        dp[0][j] = val
                        dp[1][j] = dp[2][j] = 0
                    continue
                best0 = ...
                best1 = ...
                best2 = ...
                if val >= 0:
                    dp[0][j] = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG
                    dp[1][j] = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
                    dp[2][j] = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
                else:
                    new0 = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG
                    new1 = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
                    if best0 != NEG and best0 > new1:
                        new1 = best0
                    new2 = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
                    if best1 != NEG and best1 > new2:
                        new2 = best1
                    dp[0][j] = new0
                    dp[1][j] = new1
                    dp[2][j] = new2
        return dp[2][n-1]
```

Final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumAmount(self, coins: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(coins), len(coins[0])
        NEG = -10**18

        dp0 = [NEG] * n
        dp1 = [NEG] * n
        dp2 = [NEG] * n

        for i in range(m):
            for j in range(n):
                val = coins[i][j]

                if i == 0 and j == 0:
                    if val >= 0:
                        dp0[j] = val
                        dp1[j] = NEG
                        dp2[j] = NEG
                    else:
                        dp0[j] = val
                        dp1[j] = 0
                        dp2[j] = NEG
                    continue

                best0 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best0 = dp0[j]
                if j > 0 and dp0[j - 1] > best0:
                    best0 = dp0[j - 1]

                best1 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best1 = dp1[j]
                if j > 0 and dp1[j - 1] > best1:
                    best1 = dp1[j - 1]

                best2 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best2 = dp2[j]
                if j > 0 and dp2[j - 1] > best2:
                    best2 = dp2[j - 1]

                if val >= 0:
                    dp0[j] = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG
                    dp1[j] = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
                    dp2[j] = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
                else:
                    new0 = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG

                    new1 = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
                    if best0 != NEG and best0 > new1:
                        new1 = best0

                    new2 = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
                    if best1 != NEG and best1 > new2:
                        new2 = best1

                    dp0[j] = new0
                    dp1[j] = new1
                    dp2[j] = new2

        return max(dp0[n - 1], dp1[n - 1], dp2[n - 1])
```

Let's think if there is any issue with "exactly k neutralizations" and start negative: dp2=NEG. But if there are two negative cells and start is one, dp2 reachable at second. Good.

Potential issue: If val>=0 and best1 is NEG, dp1[j]=NEG. But if val>=0 and best1 is NEG, but best0 is reachable, could we have used a neutralization on a previous negative and then current nonnegative? That would be exact1, so best1 would be reachable. If no previous negative, exact1 unreachable. Good.

Potential issue: If val<0 and best1 is NEG, new1=best0. If best0 is NEG, new1=NEG. But best0 should be reachable. Good.

Let's maybe think if there is any issue with "at most 2 cells" and exact state if the robot can choose to neutralize a robber in a cell but not use all ability; final max all. Good.

Let's maybe think if there is any issue with "m,n up to 500" and O(mn) time. Good.

Potential issue: If the platform uses Python 3.9, type annotations okay. Good.

Now, let's decide final code. I will use exact state with manual best0/best1/best2, not list allocation, to be slightly more efficient and clear. The code is a bit longer but fine. Let's ensure indentation.

Final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumAmount(self, coins: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(coins), len(coins[0])
        NEG = -10**18

        dp0 = [NEG] * n
        dp1 = [NEG] * n
        dp2 = [NEG] * n

        for i in range(m):
            for j in range(n):
                val = coins[i][j]

                if i == 0 and j == 0:
                    if val >= 0:
                        dp0[j] = val
                        dp1[j] = NEG
                        dp2[j] = NEG
                    else:
                        dp0[j] = val
                        dp1[j] = 0
                        dp2[j] = NEG
                    continue

                best0 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best0 = dp0[j]
                if j > 0 and dp0[j - 1] > best0:
                    best0 = dp0[j - 1]

                best1 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best1 = dp1[j]
                if j > 0 and dp1[j - 1] > best1:
                    best1 = dp1[j - 1]

                best2 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best2 = dp2[j]
                if j > 0 and dp2[j - 1] > best2:
                    best2 = dp2[j - 1]

                if val >= 0:
                    dp0[j] = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG
                    dp1[j] = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
                    dp2[j] = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
                else:
                    new0 = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG

                    new1 = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
                    if best0 != NEG and best0 > new1:
                        new1 = best0

                    new2 = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
                    if best1 != NEG and best1 > new2:
                        new2 = best1

                    dp0[j] = new0
                    dp1[j] = new1
                    dp2[j] = new2

        return max(dp0[n - 1], dp1[n - 1], dp2[n - 1])
```

Potential issue: If val>=0 and best1 is NEG, dp1[j]=NEG. But if val>=0 and best1 is NEG, but best0 is reachable, could we have used a neutralization on a previous negative and then current nonnegative? That would be exact1, so best1 would be reachable. If no previous negative, exact1 unreachable. Good.

Potential issue: If val<0 and best1 is NEG, new1=best0. If best0 is NEG, new1=NEG. But best0 should be reachable. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
} from './response';
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumAmount(self, coins: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(coins), len(coins[0])
        NEG = -10**18

        # dp0/dp1/dp2: best profit at the current row/column
        # after using exactly 0/1/2 neutralizations.
        dp0 = [NEG] * n
        dp1 = [NEG] * n
        dp2 = [NEG] * n

        for i in range(m):
            for j in range(n):
                val = coins[i][j]

                if i == 0 and j == 0:
                    if val >= 0:
                        dp0[j] = val
                        dp1[j] = NEG
                        dp2[j] = NEG
                    else:
                        dp0[j] = val
                        dp1[j] = 0
                        dp2[j] = NEG
                    continue

                best0 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best0 = dp0[j]
                if j > 0 and dp0[j - 1] > best0:
                    best0 = dp0[j - 1]

                best1 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best1 = dp1[j]
                if j > 0 and dp1[j - 1] > best1:
                    best1 = dp1[j - 1]

                best2 = NEG
                if i > 0:
                    best2 = dp2[j]
                if j > 0 and dp2[j - 1] > best2:
                    best2 = dp2[j - 1]

                if val >= 0:
                    dp0[j] = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG
                    dp1[j] = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
                    dp2[j] = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
                else:
                    new0 = best0 + val if best0 != NEG else NEG

                    new1 = best1 + val if best1 != NEG else NEG
                    if best0 != NEG and best0 > new1:
                        new1 = best0

                    new2 = best2 + val if best2 != NEG else NEG
                    if best1 != NEG and best1 > new2:
                        new2 = best1

                    dp0[j] = new0
                    dp1[j] = new1
                    dp2[j] = new2

        return max(dp0[n - 1], dp1[n - 1], dp2[n - 1])
```
